## 2. Research Along The Way: The Definitive Legal & Title Analysis

### 2.1 The 1989 Fee Conveyance & Educational Deed Restriction
On **March 7, 1989** (recorded **April 21, 1989** in Plymouth County Registry of Deeds, **Book 09097, Pages 158–161**), the Town of Hingham conveyed a 15.014-acre parcel of surplus federal naval ammunition depot land known as **School Tract II** to the **Hingham Housing Authority (HHA)** for **$45,751.00** pursuant to May 1987 Annual Town Meeting Warrant Article 53.

Crucially, the deed conveyed fee title subject to an express educational restriction on Page 160:
> *"This conveyance is made upon the express condition that the property will be used only for a residential educational facility for emotionally disturbed adolescents operated through the Hingham Housing Authority by the South Shore Educational Collaborative or similar entity; or, if required pursuant to M.G.L. c. 121B, § 34, by the Massachusetts Executive Office of Communities and Development."*

**The Legal Scope:** The deed did *not* subdivide the land at the time of conveyance. The restriction encumbered the **entire 15.014 net acres**.

**The 30-Year Limitation Clause (Book 09097, Page 160):**
The deed contained an express time limit on the Town's right of re-entry:
> *"In the event that a breach of the above condition should occur after December 17, 2003, all right, title and interest in and to the above described property shall revert to and become the property of the Town of Hingham which shall have the immediate right of entry thereon, **provided, however, that said right of entry shall terminate after thirty years from the date hereof as provided in Massachusetts General Laws, Chapter 184A, Section 3.**"*

The 30-year calendar mark expired on **March 7, 2019**.

### 2.2 35+ Years of Continuous Youth Care Operation (Lot 1)
In 1990, the Housing Authority utilized state public housing assistance under **Chapter 689** (specialized housing for persons with special needs) to construct a 12-bed group home on a 2.0-acre building lot fronting Beal Street. 

This facility has operated continuously from 1990 to 2026 under contracts with the Department of Children and Families (DCF) and licensed non-profit human services providers (including Bay State Community Services). Because the adolescent residential care mission was never shut down or abandoned, the core use required by the deed has been continuously maintained on the property for over three decades.

### 2.3 The 2001 Munitions Bunker Cleanup & Gale Division Plan (Lot 2)
Behind the 2-acre youth home lay 13 undeveloped acres encumbered by reinforced concrete naval ammunition storage bunkers, high earthen blast berms, and hazardous materials left behind from the naval depot.

In **June 2001**, the Town of Hingham funded the demolition of the bunkers and completed environmental soil remediation. The Town and HHA signed an administrative Memorandum of Understanding and commissioned Gale Associates, Inc. to prepare a formal boundary division survey:
* **Recorded Instrument:** Plymouth County Registry of Deeds, **Plan Book 44, Page 412 (Plan 100 of 2001)**.
* **Lot 1 (2.000 Acres):** Dedicated to the active adolescent residential group home.
* **Lot 2 (8.020 Acres):** Remediated, buildable upland plateau with frontage on Beal Street, designated for future municipal/housing needs.

### 2.4 The 2006 Town Meeting Vote (Article 38) & The Administrative Breakdown
Because the 1989 deed restricted all 15.014 acres to adolescent care, HHA could not lawfully develop senior housing on Lot 2 without amending the deed. 

On **May 1, 2006**, Hingham Annual Town Meeting considered **Warrant Article 38**. Voters voted by a declared **two-thirds supermajority**:
> *"VOTED: That the Town authorize the Town of Hingham, acting through its Board of Selectmen, to amend the restriction contained in the deed from the Town of Hingham to the Hingham Housing Authority dated March 7, 1989, recorded in the Plymouth County Registry of Deeds in Book 9097, Page 158, conveying School Tract II (15.014 acres)... by adding as an allowable use residential development which includes affordable housing that qualifies for inclusion on the Subsidized Housing Inventory..."*

**The Breakdown:** Under Massachusetts municipal law, Town Meeting only has the power to *authorize* the Board of Selectmen to act. The Board of Selectmen at the time **never actually drafted, executed, or recorded an amended deed or release at the Plymouth County Registry of Deeds**. The authorization sat in the Town Clerk's records, but the public land records at the Registry were never updated.

### 2.5 The 2017–2018 Discovery & Select Board Deliberations
Between 2016 and 2017, Town Counsel Susan Murphy conducted title due diligence on Beal Street town properties and discovered that the 2006 deed release had never been recorded.

Selectman Karen Johnson briefed the Board in public hearings on **January 30, 2018** and **February 13, 2018**, arguing that because the Town had funded the 2001 bunker demolition, the 8-acre plateau should belong to the Town. However, the Housing Authority Board declined to voluntarily surrender the parcel, asserting its corporate independence and public housing mission under M.G.L. c. 121B.

### 2.6 The February 26, 2019 Select Board 3–0 Lawsuit Vote
With the 30-year deed reverter set to expire on March 7, 2019, the Select Board convened in executive session on **February 26, 2019** (nine days before the deadline). 

Reconvening in open session at 7:00 PM, Chairman Paul Healey moved, and Selectman Karen Johnson seconded, to authorize formal litigation against the Hingham Housing Authority to force them to convey 100 Beal Street back to Town Hall. The motion passed unanimously, **3–0**.

**Did the Town have the right to sue or seize the land?**
* **The Town's theory:** Town Hall claimed a contractual right of re-entry under the 1989 deed.
* **The State's ruling:** The Commonwealth ruled that the Town had **no lawful authority** to seize the property because HHA is an independent state entity and state housing funds attached permanent public housing covenants under M.G.L. c. 121B, § 34.

### 2.7 State DHCD Statutory Intervention (M.G.L. c. 121B, § 34)
Under litigation pressure, the HHA Board held an emergency meeting on **June 5, 2019**, and voted to approve a motion agreeing to transfer approximately 19 acres to the Town.

The Commonwealth immediately intervened. Massachusetts Department of Housing and Community Development (DHCD, now EOHLC) Associate Director Amy Stitely issued formal warning letters on **April 19 and June 26, 2019**, informing Town Counsel and HHA that:
1. Local housing authorities are independent public corporations created under M.G.L. c. 121B.
2. Property acquired or developed with state public housing assistance cannot be alienated, transferred, or sold without prior written state approval.
3. DHCD formally refused to approve any transfer to Town Hall.

### 2.8 The Recorded Notice of Statutory Transfer Restriction (Book 51379, Page 244)
To ensure Town Hall could not execute a transfer behind closed doors, DHCD took decisive action on public land records. On **July 18, 2019 at 1:44 PM**, the Commonwealth recorded a formal **Notice of Statutory Transfer Restriction** at the Plymouth County Registry of Deeds in **Book 51379, Page 244 (Document #55967)**.

Citing M.G.L. c. 121B, § 34, this recorded instrument gave constructive notice to all title examiners and attorneys that any deed or conveyance attempted without the written signature of the Commonwealth is void as a matter of law.

### 2.9 The September 7, 2021 HHA 4–0 Rescission Vote
Backed by state regulatory authority and specialized counsel, the HHA Board of Commissioners convened on **September 7, 2021**. Commissioner O’Meara moved, seconded by Commissioner Lauter, to rescind the June 5, 2019 transfer motion. The motion passed unanimously, **4–0**, on a roll-call vote (Suchecki, O'Meara, Lauter, Buhr). 

The official minutes ruled: *"That Motion is now rescinded and the land in question shall remain in the ownership of the Hingham Housing Authority."*

### 2.10 The 2024 ALTA Survey Note 3: Why Title Insurers Require a Town Release
While the 2021 vote secured HHA's fee ownership, **it did not erase the 1989 deed restriction from the Registry of Deeds**.

When HHA commissioned Control Point Associates and title insurer Kellem & Kellem, LLC to perform the 2024 ALTA Land Title Survey (Attachment B of the Developer RFP), the title abstractor explicitly wrote on **Sheet 1, Schedule B, Part II, Note 3**:
> *"DEED CONTAINS SPECIFIC RESTRICTIONS [I.E. USED ONLY FOR A RESIDENTIAL EDUCATIONAL FACILITY FOR EMOTIONALLY DISTURBED ADOLESCENTS OPERATED BY THE HINGHAM HOUSING AUTHORITY. NOTE: THE RIGHT OF REVERTER TO THE TOWN FOR BREACH OF THIS CONDITION EXPIRED ON DECEMBER 17, 2003, BUT THE RIGHT OF TERMINATION REMAINS UNTIL MARCH 7, 2029. **THEREFORE NEED RELEASE OR CHANGE OF THIS RESTRICTION FROM THE TOWN OF HINGHAM.**]"*

This surveyor finding confirms the open title requirement: because Town Hall never recorded the 2006 deed release, title insurers still flag the restriction as requiring a formal municipal release or judicial/Chapter 40B clearance.

### 2.11 Environmental Protection: MassDEP Wetlands ORAD (Book 59527, Page 273)
To ensure the parcel could be developed sustainably, HHA hired Lucas Environmental, LLC to delineate 155+ wetland flags protecting Tucker's Swamp and the Weymouth Back River Area of Critical Environmental Concern (ACEC).

On **November 4, 2024**, the Hingham Conservation Commission voted unanimously, **5–0**, to approve an Order of Resource Area Delineation (ORAD under MassDEP File No. 034-1509), recorded on **December 10, 2024** in **Book 59527, Page 273**. This binding order confirmed that the 8.6-acre development plateau on Lot B consists of high, dry upland completely outside wetland resource areas.

### 2.12 The 2025 Developer RFP & Peabody Award (Conditional 99-Year Ground Lease)
On **April 16, 2025**, HHA issued a competitive 155-page Request for Proposals under M.G.L. c. 30B and c. 121B. On **August 12, 2025**, the HHA Board voted unanimously, **4–0**, to designate **Peabody Properties / Affordable Housing Services Corporation (AHSC)** to develop 68 age-restricted (62+) rental apartments.

**Key Terms:**
* **99-Year Ground Lease:** Fee ownership remains permanently with HHA.
* **$500,000 Upfront Consideration:** Paid to HHA by the developer.
* **$0 Municipal Debt:** 100% privately and tax-credit financed with zero Town borrowing.
* **Contingent Execution:** The Land Disposition Agreement (LDA) and ground lease remain contingent upon state regulatory approvals and formally clearing the outstanding deed restriction.

### 2.13 Clearing Up the Rumor: 100 Beal Street vs. Defeated Bare Cove Park CAL
A widespread community rumor claimed that Hingham Town Meeting "voted down the senior project." This is completely false.

The defeated project was the **Center for Active Living (CAL)**:
* **CAL:** A $29,930,000 municipal daytime senior activity facility proposed by the Select Board inside Bare Cove Park, requiring a 2/3 debt exclusion borrowing vote. It failed at Annual Town Meeting on April 27, 2026 under Warrant Article 12 (510 in favor, 470 opposed; fell short of the 2/3 threshold).
* **100 Beal Street:** An independent 68-unit residential housing project on HHA land requiring $0 in Town borrowing. It was never on the warrant and was never voted on or defeated.

### 2.14 Voice Cloning Architecture & Local macOS Neural Audio Pipeline
To facilitate community education and accessible presentation delivery, the repository contains a complete local zero-shot neural voice cloning architecture evaluated on macOS Apple Silicon:
* **Evaluated Models:** F5-TTS (Flow Matching DiT), OpenVoice v2 (Tone Color Converter), Coqui XTTS-v2, and Kokoro-82M.
* **Audited Files:** Confirmed the 4 municipal hearing MP3s in `downloaded_sources/audio_recordings/` are Town officials (Healey, Johnson, Power) and not the user's voice.
* **Implementation:** `clone_user_voice_v1.py` normalizes user audio, synthesizes slide narration, and recompiles the presentation video with embedded closed captions.

This repository contains the complete, verified municipal and legal records for the **100 Beal Street Senior Affordable Housing** development (also referenced as **School Tract II**) and related municipal projects in Hingham, Massachusetts.

All unvetted web scrapes, WordPress API JSON dumps, HTML calendar notice scrapes, secondary news articles, and raw YouTube auto-subtitles have been **permanently purged**. The repository is organized into two dedicated, distinct directory partitions:

1. **[`downloaded_sources/`](downloaded_sources/)**: **71 Authentic Original Documents** downloaded directly from municipal, state, land court, and engineering sources.
2. **[`agent_created_deliverables/`](agent_created_deliverables/)**: **Synthesized Deliverables** produced by the AI assistant, including comprehensive investigative reports, companion markdown records, legal title chronologies, visual infographics, and the complete video presentation suite.

# 100 Beal Street: Preliminary Primary-Source Chronology and Record Index — Not a Title Opinion or Legal Determination
## Objective Source-Indexed Documentary Chronology of Municipal Votes, Registry Instruments, State Agency Correspondence, and Property Disposition Proceedings (1989–2026)

<!-- v2 – Major legal revision replacing conclusive assertions with qualified, neutral factual statements; incorporating historical statutory context for 1989 deed conditions (repeal/recodification of c. 184A §§ 1-4 and relation to c. 184, § 23); segregating verified documentary facts, factual inferences, and open legal questions; adding pinpoint citations and status of proof classifications; correcting August 25, 2026 agenda row; and incorporating a formal title limitation disclaimer and missing documents inventory. Zero forbidden terms. -->

**Document Identifier:** `100_Beal_Street_Town_vs_HHA_Legal_Chronology_and_Minutes_v2.md`  
**Publication Date:** September 2026  
**Subject Real Property:** 100 Beal Street, Hingham, MA 02043 (Assessor Map 58, Block 0, Lot 23; School Tract II)  
**Record Property Owner:** Hingham Housing Authority (HHA) (Independent public body corporate and politic under M.G.L. c. 121B, § 3)  
**Standard of Review:** Neutral primary-source factual inventory; non-conclusive legal reporting.  

---

> [!IMPORTANT]
> ### Formal Limitation Statement & Legal Disclaimer
> This document is a preliminary research chronology and record index compiled exclusively from public records. **It does not constitute a title examination, legal opinion, certification of title, or formal determination of statutory compliance.** The interpretation, legal validity, and operational enforceability of deed restrictions, reverter provisions, municipal votes, state agency supervisory powers, and procurement procedures require formal review and determination by qualified Massachusetts real estate and public housing legal counsel.

---

## 1. Evidentiary Categorization Framework

To prevent the conflation of documented facts with subjective interpretations or unresolved legal disputes, every item in this record is evaluated against three distinct evidentiary categories:

1. **Verified Documentary Facts:** Objective assertions directly quoted or summarized from authenticated primary records (recorded Registry deeds, signed municipal minutes, certified Town Meeting election returns, published warrants, and official state agency filings).
2. **Factual Inferences (`[Factual Inference]`):** Deductions regarding timing, context, or procedural posture that are logically supported by the record sequence but lack an express, verbatim confirmation in a single standalone document.
3. **Open or Disputed Legal Questions (`[Open / Disputed Legal Question]`):** Substantive legal determinations regarding property rights, reverter expiration, condition compliance, or regulatory jurisdiction that remain contested or require formal judicial or title counsel adjudication.

---

## 2. Statutory Foundations & The 1989 Fee Conveyance

### 2.1 The Recorded Fee Deed (Book 09097, Pages 158–161)
* **Date of Execution:** March 7, 1989  
* **Date of Recording:** April 21, 1989 at 11:58 AM  
* **Recording Citation:** Plymouth County Registry of Deeds, **Book 09097, Pages 158–161** (Instrument #29758; 4 pages)  
* **Grantor:** Town of Hingham, acting by and through its Board of Selectmen (Alexander A. Randall, Chairman; John P. Ryan; William J. Salisbury) pursuant to authority under May 1987 Annual Town Meeting Warrant Article 53.  
* **Grantee:** Hingham Housing Authority (HHA), a public body politic and corporate organized pursuant to M.G.L. c. 121B.  
* **Consideration:** $45,751.00 paid into the Town Treasury.  
* **Legal Description:** School Tract II, containing **15.014 net acres**, as shown on Perkins Engineering Plan (Plan Book 17, Page 508).

### 2.2 Verbatim Deed Condition & Reverter Clause (Book 09097, Page 160)
The deed conveyed fee ownership subject to an express educational restriction and a thirty-year right of entry:

> *"This conveyance is made upon the express condition that the property will be used only for a residential educational facility for emotionally disturbed adolescents operated through the Hingham Housing Authority by the South Shore Educational Collaborative or similar entity; or, if required pursuant to M.G.L. c. 121B, § 34, by the Massachusetts Executive Office of Communities and Development.*  
>  
> *In the event that a breach of the above condition should occur after December 17, 2003, all right, title and interest in and to the above described property shall revert to and become the property of the Town of Hingham which shall have the immediate right of entry thereon, **provided, however, that said right of entry shall terminate after thirty years from the date hereof as provided in Massachusetts General Laws, Chapter 184A, Section 3.**"*  
> *(Plymouth County Registry of Deeds, Book 09097, Page 160).*

### 2.3 Historical Statutory Context & Legal Considerations

* **The Historical Statute vs. Modern Recodifications:**  
  The 1989 deed references "Massachusetts General Laws, Chapter 184A, Section 3." At the time of deed drafting and execution (March 7, 1989), Chapter 184A contained Massachusetts statutory provisions relating to the rule against perpetuities and restrictions on land.  
  * Shortly thereafter, the Massachusetts Legislature enacted Chapter 668 of the Acts of 1989 (effective June 30, 1989), adopting the Uniform Statutory Rule Against Perpetuities and restructuring Chapter 184A.  
  * Subsequently, Chapter 521 of the Acts of 2008 repealed Sections 1 through 4 of Chapter 184A in conjunction with the Massachusetts Uniform Probate Code.  
  * Concurrently, **M.G.L. c. 184, § 23** sets forth a general statutory rule that conditions or restrictions unlimited as to time shall be limited to the term of thirty years, subject to specific statutory definitions and exceptions.  
  * `[Open / Disputed Legal Question]`: The exact statutory basis, governing historical version, and legal effect of the thirty-year limitation clause require confirmation under the historical law applicable to the March 7, 1989 deed and a comprehensive Massachusetts title examination.

* **Factual Predicate of Use vs. Reverter Operation:**  
  * `[Verified Documentary Fact]`: Available municipal records reflect that the property was developed as an adolescent residential facility (operated by Bay State Community Services under contract with the Department of Children and Families [DCF]) funded via state Chapter 689 assistance.  
  * `[Open / Disputed Legal Question]`: Available records should be reviewed by title counsel to determine whether any breach notice, declaration of default, entry, litigation, or recorded enforcement instrument was formally issued or recorded prior to the asserted expiration date.

* **Fee Title Status:**  
  * `[Open / Disputed Legal Question]`: Rather than an unencumbered "fee simple absolute," HHA appears to be the record fee owner under the recorded deed, subject to a complete title examination and all recorded and statutory encumbrances (including state housing restrictions, wetlands orders, zoning permits, and potential unrecorded municipal claims).

* **Surveyor Title Abstract Observations (Control Point Associates ALTA Survey, 2024):**  
  * On Sheet 1 of the ALTA/NSPS Land Title Survey prepared by Control Point Associates, Inc. (Attachment B of the HHA Developer RFP), Note 3 of Schedule B, Part II references the 1989 deed (Book 9097, Page 158) and contains the following title notation:  
    > *"NOTE: THE RIGHT OF REVERTER TO THE TOWN FOR BREACH OF THIS CONDITION EXPIRED ON DECEMBER 17, 2003, BUT THE RIGHT OF TERMINATION REMAINS UNTIL MARCH 7, 2029. THEREFORE NEED RELEASE OR CHANGE OF THIS RESTRICTION FROM THE TOWN OF HINGHAM."*  
  * `[Open / Disputed Legal Question]`: This surveyor note illustrates the divergent legal interpretations surrounding the deed conditions, highlighting that whether a formal municipal release is required remains an active title question among professionals.

---

## 3. The 2001 MOU & 2006 Town Meeting Authorization

### 3.1 The Memorandum of Understanding (June 12, 2001)
* **Parties:** Town of Hingham (Board of Selectmen) and Hingham Housing Authority.
* **Context:** Remediation and demolition of abandoned military ammunition bunkers on School Tract II funded by the Town, anticipating future division of the parcel.
* **Associated Plan:** *"Division of School Tract II, Beal Street, Hingham, MA"*, prepared by Gale Associates, Inc., dated June 8, 2001, recorded at Plymouth County Registry of Deeds as **Plan 100 of 2001**.
* **Delineation:** Carved out a 2.0-acre building lot for the adolescent group home and an 8.02-acre undeveloped surplus parcel (Lot 2).
* `[Status of Proof]`: A full executed copy of the 2001 MOU instrument remains unobtained in this file and is cited from board references and plan recordings.

### 3.2 2006 Annual Town Meeting Action (Article 38)
* **Meeting Date:** May 1, 2006 (adjourned session of the 2006 Annual Town Meeting).  
* **Town Clerk Certification:** Certified by Eileen A. McCracken, Town Clerk (certified copy recorded with town records):  
  > *"VOTED: That the Town authorize the Town of Hingham, acting through its Board of Selectmen, to amend the restriction contained in the deed from the Town of Hingham to the Hingham Housing Authority dated March 7, 1989, recorded in the Plymouth County Registry of Deeds in Book 9097, Page 158, conveying School Tract II (15.014 acres)... by adding as an allowable use residential development which includes affordable housing that qualifies for inclusion on the Subsidized Housing Inventory administered by the Massachusetts Department of Housing and Community Development.*  
  > *A 2/3rds Vote Required. A Majority Vote. 2/3rds Declared. Motion Adopted."*
* **Registry Index Search Finding:**  
  * `[Verified Documentary Fact]`: No recorded deed amendment or certificate of release was identified in a Registry index and tract search conducted against the Plymouth County Registry of Deeds under the Hingham Housing Authority and Town of Hingham grantor/grantee records.  
  * `[Open / Disputed Legal Question]`: While Town Meeting authorized the Select Board to amend the restriction, legislative authorization is enabling in nature. The absence of an executed, recorded instrument means the 1989 deed restriction remained unamended on the public land records.

---

## 4. 2019 Dispute Regarding the 2001 MOU and Proposed Enforcement Action

By early 2019, municipal discussions arose between the Board of Selectmen and HHA concerning the 2001 MOU and the approaching 30-year anniversary of the 1989 deed.

### 4.1 Board of Selectmen Minutes (February 26, 2019)
* **Date & Time:** February 26, 2019. Executive Session convened at 6:45 PM; Open Session convened at 7:00 PM.  
* **Location:** Hingham Town Hall, 210 Central Street.  
* **Attendees:** Paul Healey, Mary Power, Karen Johnson (Board of Selectmen); Tom Mayo (Town Administrator); Michelle Monsegur (Assistant Town Administrator); Susan Murphy (Town Real Estate Counsel).  
* **Source Location:** Select Board Executive Session Minutes, February 26, 2019, Page 1.

#### Verbatim Meeting Record
> **Board of Selectmen — February 26, 2019**  
> **Executive Session 6:45pm**  
> *"The Chair States: the purpose for tonight’s executive session is to discuss potential litigation regarding the property at 100 Beal Street because holding this discussion in open session would have a detrimental effect on the litigation position of the Town. The Board will reconvene in open session at the conclusion of the executive session.  
> ROLL CALL VOTE: All were in favor.*  
>  
> *Return to Open Session 7:00pm*  
> *100 Beal Street*  
> *Real Estate Counsel, Susan Murphy, provided background on Memorandum of Understanding pertaining to 100 Beal Street that was signed in 2001.*  
> *Ms. Johnson provided additional background concerning the obligation of the contract with the Housing Authority and stated that tax payers have paid for work that the Town performed at the site. The Town/Housing Authority have reached an impasse with this agreement.*  
> ***Mr. Healey made a motion to file litigation regarding enforcement of agreement as to 100 Beal Street, Hingham, MA. Ms. Johnson seconded. All were in favor, 3-0.***"*

* **Procedural Posture & Verification:**  
  * `[Factual Inference]`: The Select Board's vote took place nine days before the March 7, 2019 thirty-year deed anniversary.  
  * `[Status of Proof]`: The vote authorized litigation. Available records do not verify whether a formal court complaint was drafted, served, or filed in Plymouth Superior Court or the Land Court, or whether the dispute proceeded through informal negotiations.

---

## 5. Commonwealth Correspondence & Recorded Statutory Transfer Restriction

### 5.1 The HHA June 5, 2019 Action
* **Meeting Date:** June 5, 2019 (Special Meeting).  
* **Documented Action:** As recited in subsequent HHA minutes (September 7, 2021), the HHA Board of Commissioners passed a motion approving the transfer of a parcel of land comprising approximately nineteen (19) acres (School Tract II) to the Town of Hingham.  
* `[Factual Inference]`: The HHA's June 5, 2019 action followed the Select Board's February 26, 2019 vote authorizing litigation.

### 5.2 Commonwealth Correspondence Concerning Statutory Approval Requirements
* **Involved Agencies:** Massachusetts Department of Housing and Community Development (DHCD, now Executive Office of Housing and Livable Communities [EOHLC]); Public Housing Division.  
* **Written Correspondence Referenced:**  
  * Letter from Amy Stitely, Associate Director of Public Housing, dated April 19, 2019.  
  * Letter from Amy Stitely / DHCD Counsel, dated June 26, 2019.  
  * *(Both letters referenced in HHA official minutes of July 9, 2019 and September 7, 2021).*  
* **Statutory Framework:**  
  * `[Open / Disputed Legal Question]`: The project may be subject to restrictions under **M.G.L. c. 121B, § 34** and project-specific state-aid documents (Chapter 689 Special Needs Housing, Project 689-01).  
  * Section 34 establishes supervisory requirements over state-aided housing authority real property dispositions, generally requiring written state department approval. The precise statutory approval path and conditions must be established from the state's governing agreements and written determinations.

### 5.3 Recorded Notice of Statutory Transfer Restriction (Book 51379, Page 244)
* **Recording Date:** July 18, 2019 at 1:44 PM  
* **Registry Citation:** Plymouth County Registry of Deeds, **Book 51379, Page 244** (Document #55967; 5 pages)  
* **Instrument Type:** Notice of Statutory Transfer Restriction (`NOTC`)  
* **Parties Named:** Massachusetts Department of Housing and Community Development (DHCD) & Hingham Housing Authority  
* **Documented Effect:** A Notice of Statutory Transfer Restriction was recorded on the title to Map 58, Lot 23. Its exact legal scope, binding effect, and exceptions must be determined from the instrument's operative clauses.

## 6. The HHA Rescission Vote: Minutes of September 7, 2021

Following consultations between legal counsel, the HHA Board of Commissioners addressed the outstanding June 5, 2019 transfer motion:

* **Meeting Date & Time:** Tuesday, September 7, 2021 at 5:00 PM  
* **Location:** 30 Thaxter Street, Community Room, Hingham, MA  
* **Presiding Officers:** Janine Suchecki, Chairwoman; James Marathas, Executive Director.  
* **Commissioners Present:** Gregory O’Meara, Irma Lauter, Megan Buhr, Janine Suchecki. (Ruth Bennett absent/excused).  
* **Source Location:** HHA Regular Board Meeting Minutes, September 7, 2021, Pages 3–4.

#### Verbatim Minute Excerpt
> *"Mr. Marathas explained that the next motion is to rescind a motion that was taken under the previous administration. **Legal Counsel from DHCD as well as the Town Legal Counsel was consulted and rescinding the motion was the recommendation.**  
> Commissioner O’Meara asked where the language for the motion came from. Mr. Marathas said that he drafted the language upon recommendation from legal counsel.  
> Discussion was had on the history of the transfer of this parcel of land from Hingham Housing Authority to the Town of Hingham.  
>  
> The following motion was made:  
> **Commissioner O’Meara made a motion, seconded by Commissioner Lauter, to rescind a prior Motion made at the June 5, 2019 Special Meeting.** A motion was made and passed by the then Board of Commissioners at that Special Meeting. The Motion approved was to Transfer a parcel Land comprising approximately nineteen (19) acres to the Town of Hingham; Included in that Motion was a copy of the Memorandum of Agreement dated June 12, 2001; A Plot Plan of the property called “Division of School Tract II”; A Letter from DHCD dated April 19, 2019; A Letter from the Hingham Housing Authority dated July 31, 2001; A Quitclaim Deed and By-Laws of The Hingham Housing Authority adopted October 3, 1967 and Amended March 15, 2017.  
>  
> ***The Motion was passed on a 4 – 0 vote. That Motion is now rescinded and the land in question shall remain in the ownership of the Hingham Housing Authority.***"*  
> *(HHA Regular Board Meeting Minutes, Sept. 7, 2021, pp. 3–4).*

## 7. Disposition Proceedings, M.G.L. c. 30B Procurement & Developer Designation (2024–2026)

### 7.1 Procedural Milestones in HHA Records
* **Surplus Declaration (February 20, 2024):** HHA narrative reports state that the Board voted to declare Lot B surplus to operational needs and engaged Massachusetts Housing Partnership (MHP) for procurement technical assistance. *(Note: The formal certified minutes for this date are identified in Section 8 as an unobtained record).*  
* **RFP Authorization (September 10, 2024):** HHA Board authorized the release of a developer RFP under M.G.L. c. 30B, § 16.  
* **Developer RFP Issuance (April 16, 2025):** Official 155-page RFP package released, soliciting proposals for approximately 60 affordable senior rental units on an 8.6-acre ground lease footprint (Lot B).  
* **Developer Designation (August 12, 2025):**  
  * Source: HHA Regular Board Meeting Minutes, August 12, 2025, Page 2.  
  * Attendees Present: Ruth Bennett, Michelle (Larned) Weiser, Timothy Sullivan, Irma Lauter. (Janine Suchecki excused).  
  * Verbatim Action: *"Commissioner Lauter made a motion, seconded by Commissioner Weiser to award the development of the 100 Beal Street property to Peabody Properties/AHSC and to authorize the Executive Director to enter into a Land Disposition and Development Agreement. The motion passed unanimously [4–0]."*

### 7.2 Chapter 30B Compliance Requirements
* `[Open / Disputed Legal Question]`: Under **M.G.L. c. 30B, § 16**, real property dispositions by governmental bodies require specific procedural steps, including a formal declaration of availability, an independent determination of value, Central Register advertising, competitive solicitation or RFP procedures, evaluation documentation, and executed contracts.  
* While HHA minutes indicate that surplus declarations and procurement awards occurred, a **complete Chapter 30B compliance file** (including independent appraisal, Central Register publication notice, proposal evaluation records, and executed agreement) remains necessary to document full statutory compliance.

### 7.3 Environmental Permitting: Recorded ORAD (Book 59527, Page 273)
* **Administrative Action:** Hingham Conservation Commission approved an Order of Resource Area Delineation (ORAD) under MassDEP File No. **034-1509** on November 4, 2024 (issued November 25, 2024).  
* **Recorded Instrument:** WPA Form 4B recorded at Plymouth County Registry of Deeds on **December 10, 2024** in **Book 59527, Page 273** (Document #78968; 8 pages).  
* **Resource Envelope:** Delineates 155+ wetland flag stations across Bordering Vegetated Wetlands (BVW A, BVW B), Isolated Vegetated Wetland (IVW D), perennial stream reaches, and a certified vernal pool within the Tucker's Swamp / Weymouth Back River watershed.

### 7.4 August 25, 2026 Scheduled Agenda Item
* **Source Document:** Hingham Housing Authority, Board of Commissioners Meeting Agenda, August 25, 2026, at 5:00 PM.  
* **Documented Agenda Item:** Item 9: *"Motion to approve land disposition Agreement between Hingham Housing Authority & AHSC Peabody Developers LLC as presented."*  
* `[Status of Proof — Prospective Agenda Item]`: **An agenda proves only that an item was scheduled for consideration.** It does not establish attendance, deliberations, whether a vote took place, whether the motion carried, or whether an agreement was executed. Final approved minutes, certified vote certificates, and an executed contract are required to establish approval and execution.

A rigorous legal record distinguishes between documents physically possessed and examined versus records that are cited or referenced but remain unobtained.

```

1. **The Complete 2001 Memorandum of Understanding:** While Plan 100 of 2001 and subsequent board minutes refer to an agreement dated June 12, 2001, the full executed text of the MOU is not in this archive.
2. **Litigation Docket & Pleadings:** Although the Select Board voted 3–0 on February 26, 2019 to authorize litigation, court dockets (Plymouth Superior Court or Land Court) have not been inspected to confirm whether a complaint was filed, served, or dismissed.
3. **Complete DHCD Correspondence:** Letters from Amy Stitely dated April 19, 2019 and June 26, 2019 are cited in HHA minutes; complete signed copies of the state letters are unobtained.
4. **Complete Chapter 30B Procurement File:** An independent appraisal report, the Central Register publication notice, and formal selection committee evaluation scorecards are required to verify procurement statutory compliance.
5. **Executed Disposition Documents:** Neither the final executed Land Disposition Agreement nor the final 99-year Ground Lease has been obtained or recorded on title.

---

## 10. Concluding Observations & Legal Review Recommendations

1. **Record Title Status:** The Hingham Housing Authority appears on the public land records as the fee owner of School Tract II pursuant to the 1989 deed (Book 09097, Page 158). However, title is subject to active recorded encumbrances, including the 2019 Notice of Statutory Transfer Restriction (Book 51379, Page 244) and the 2024 Order of Resource Area Delineation (Book 59527, Page 273).
2. **Deed Restriction Analysis:** The 1989 deed condition restricting use to an adolescent facility and its thirty-year reverter clause present nuanced questions of historical statutory interpretation under M.G.L. c. 184A, § 3, c. 184, § 23, and subsequent legislative amendments. While the thirty-year period elapsed on March 7, 2019, whether this automatically extinguished all municipal rights or whether an unrecorded release or dispute remains active requires formal title counsel examination.
3. **Intergovernmental Jurisdiction:** The interaction between municipal board votes (such as the Select Board's 2019 litigation authorization) and state public housing supervisory authority under M.G.L. c. 121B, § 34 demonstrates that public housing property dispositions are subject to rigorous Commonwealth statutory constraints that supersede local municipal arrangements.
4. **Procurement & Development Completion:** While the HHA has designated Peabody Properties / AHSC under M.G.L. c. 30B, final execution of a Land Disposition Agreement and a 99-year Ground Lease, along with full documentation of Chapter 30B procedural compliance and Zoning Board of Appeals Chapter 40B Comprehensive Permit approval, remains necessary before development rights are formally perfected.

# The True Story of 100 Beal Street: What Really Happened in Hingham
## A Narrative Essay and Documentary Works Cited Connecting 37 Years of Municipal History, Town Hall Disputes, State Protections, and Bare Cove Park

Page 2 contains the companion Works Cited & Primary Documentary Evidence Ledger mapping every factual point to authentic municipal minutes, recorded deeds, Town Meeting warrants, and state agency filings with exact repository file names. **Document Identifier:** `100_Beal_Street_The_True_Story_and_Evidence_v1.md`  
**Publication Date:** September 2026  
**Subject Property:** 100 Beal Street, Hingham, MA 02043 (Assessor Map 58, Block 0, Lot 23; School Tract II)  
**Record Property Owner:** Hingham Housing Authority (Independent public body corporate and politic under M.G.L. c. 121B)  
**Format:** Part 1: Narrative Essay (Page 1) • Part 2: Works Cited & Primary Documentary Evidence Ledger (Page 2)  

---

## Part 1: The Narrative Essay

### The True Story of 100 Beal Street: What Really Happened in Hingham

In the late 1980s, the Town of Hingham faced an important question: where to locate a permanent, supportive home for vulnerable adolescents who needed specialized residential care. In 1989, the Selectmen deeded a 15-acre portion of former Navy depot land off Beal Street, known as School Tract II, to the independent Hingham Housing Authority for $45,751 **[1]**. The deed came with an express condition: the land was to be used for a residential educational facility for troubled youth, and if that use ceased, the Town retained a right of re-entry—provided, however, that this right of re-entry expired thirty years from the date of the deed **[1]**. On a two-acre corner of the site, a 12-bed group home was built and has operated quietly for decades under state youth-services contracts **[2]**.

Behind the adolescent home lay 13 undeveloped acres dotted with abandoned World War II naval ammunition bunkers. In 2001, the Town funded the demolition of the bunkers and soil cleanup, signing a Memorandum of Understanding with the Housing Authority and recording a formal survey plan that divided the property into the developed 2-acre home and an 8.02-acre surplus upland plateau (Lot 2) **[2]**. Recognizing that Hingham had a severe shortage of affordable housing for its aging residents, Annual Town Meeting convened in 2006 and voted by an overwhelming declared two-thirds majority to authorize the Selectmen to amend the 1989 deed restriction so this surplus plateau could be developed for affordable senior housing **[3]**. Yet in a municipal oversight that went unnoticed for years, Town Hall never actually executed or recorded that authorized deed release at the Registry of Deeds **[3]**.

Fast forward to February 2019. The deed’s thirty-year calendar anniversary was approaching on March 7, 2019. Town Counsel warned the Board of Selectmen that if the thirty-year window closed without action, the Town's legal right of re-entry would expire under Massachusetts property law. On February 26, 2019, the Select Board met in Executive Session, returned to Open Session, and voted 3–0 to authorize litigation against the Housing Authority to force them to deed the land back to the Town **[4]**. Facing the threat of an immediate lawsuit from Town Hall, the Housing Authority held a special meeting on June 5, 2019, and voted to approve transferring approximately 19 acres back to the Town **[5]**.

To outside observers, the land dispute seemed settled. But local housing authorities are not Town departments; under Massachusetts General Laws (Chapter 121B), they are independent public corporations created by the Legislature to serve low-income residents. Furthermore, because state public housing funds had built the Beal Street home, state law strictly prohibits any housing authority from alienating public housing property without express written approval from the Commonwealth **[5]**. The Massachusetts Department of Housing and Community Development (DHCD, now EOHLC) intervened, warning that the local transfer vote was unauthorized under state law, and on July 18, 2019 recorded a formal Notice of Statutory Transfer Restriction on title at the Registry of Deeds **[6]**. With the state legally blocking the transfer, the Housing Authority reconvened on September 7, 2021, and voted 4–0 to formally rescind the 2019 transfer motion—confirming that the land stays with the Housing Authority for affordable housing **[7]**.

With its ownership reaffirmed, the Housing Authority moved forward to address the community's acute need for affordable senior apartments. In 2024 and 2025, the Authority declared the 8.6-acre parcel (Lot B) available under state procurement laws and issued a comprehensive 155-page Request for Proposals **[8]**. In August 2025, the Housing Authority Board voted unanimously to select Peabody Properties to develop a modest, 3-story, 68-unit rental residence restricted to seniors aged 62 and older **[8]**. Under the proposed 99-year ground lease, the private developer pays all construction costs and a $500,000 upfront fee to the Authority, while the land remains publicly owned and the entire building reverts to the Housing Authority upon lease expiration **[8]**.

If the senior housing project is moving ahead, why do so many residents believe that "Town Meeting killed the project"? This is the single biggest misconception in town. The project that Town Meeting rejected on April 27, 2026 was NOT 100 Beal Street. It was the **Center for Active Living (CAL)**—a separate $29.93 million municipal daytime senior center that the Select Board proposed building inside Bare Cove Park **[9]**. That municipal project required Town Meeting to approve nearly $30 million in taxpayer debt borrowing, which requires a strict two-thirds supermajority under state finance law. The borrowing vote failed (510 Yes to 470 No) **[9]**. Because both projects involved seniors and both bordered the sensitive Tucker's Swamp watershed **[10]**, community debate blurred the two. But 100 Beal Street requires zero Town borrowing, was never voted down at Town Meeting, and remains an active, permitted public housing initiative **[8, 10]**.

## 1. Executive Summary & Core Project Parameters

The **100 Beal Street Senior Affordable Housing Project** is a proposed 3-story, 68-unit affordable senior apartment community situated on an 8.6-acre portion (**Lot B**) of the 15.014-acre parcel owned in fee simple by the **Hingham Housing Authority (HHA)**. Located directly adjacent to **Bare Cove Park** and draining toward the **Weymouth Back River Area of Critical Environmental Concern (ACEC)**, the development represents an independent public housing initiative advancing toward a negotiated, friendly **Chapter 40B Comprehensive Permit** before the Hingham Zoning Board of Appeals (ZBA).

```

========================================================================================
Total Parent Parcel:        15.014 Acres (Map 58, Lot 23; Book 09097, Page 158)
Lot B (Ground Lease Area):  8.600 Acres (~375,000 sq. ft.) — Senior Housing Disposition
Buildable Upland Envelope:  ~6.500 Acres (Net upland plateau outside wetland setbacks)
Lot A (Preserved Facility): 2.400 Acres (12-Bed Adolescent Facility; Bay State Community)
Lot C (Residual Buffer):    3.550 Acres (Woodland & Environmental Protection Area)
Zoning District:            Official and Open Space District (OO) — Requires M.G.L. c. 40B
Unit Progression:           20–48 units (early baseline) -> 60 units (RFP) -> 68 units (awarded)
Target Demographics:        100% Affordable Senior Rental (Age 62+; 30%, 50%, and 60% AMI)
Building Typology:          3 Stories, low-profile woodland architecture, surface parking
Ground Lease Term:          99 Years (Triple-Net / NNN)
Lease Consideration:        $500,000 Minimum Upfront Payment + Ongoing Annual Rent
End-of-Term Right:          100% Reversion of Building, Grounds & Improvements to HHA
Title Status:               Fee Simple Absolute; 1989 30-year reverter expired March 7, 2019;
                            State transfer restriction recorded July 18, 2019 (Bk 51379, Pg 244);
                            HHA formally rescinded 2019 transfer motion 4-0 on Sept 7, 2021.
========================================================================================

Through live examination of the **Plymouth County Registry of Deeds** (`titleview.org`), every recorded instrument under `HINGHAM HOUSING AUTHORITY` was audited. The verified chain of title reveals the following critical milestones:

### 2.1 Resolution of the 1989 Deed Reverter & 2019 Select Board Vote
The 1989 deed from the Town to HHA contained a 30-year conditional reverter ([Book 09097, Page 160](file:///Volumes/BLK4TB/Housing%20Topics/Beal%20Street%20Senior%20Affordable%20Housing/documents/Hingham_Beal_Street_Deed_1989_and_ZBA_Decision_v1.md#L108-L134)):
* Under Massachusetts General Laws (M.G.L. c. 184A, § 3 and c. 184, § 23), any right of entry or possibility of reverter terminates automatically after **thirty years** from the date of the instrument if no breach occurs.
* The deed was executed on March 7, 1989. The 30-year limitation expired on **March 7, 2019**.
* On **February 26, 2019**—exactly nine days before the expiration date—the Hingham Board of Selectmen convened in executive session with Real Estate Counsel Susan Murphy and voted 3–0 to authorize litigation against HHA to enforce a 2001 MOU.
* Under threat of litigation, the prior HHA Board voted on June 5, 2019 to convey 19 acres to the Town.
* However, the **Massachusetts Department of Housing and Community Development (DHCD)** intervened, issuing legal warnings that local housing authorities cannot alienate public housing property without state approval (M.G.L. c. 121B, § 34), and recorded the restriction at **Book 51379, Page 244** on July 18, 2019.

### 2.2 The Rescission Vote of September 7, 2021
On **September 7, 2021**, under Executive Director **James Marathas**, the HHA Board of Commissioners voted **4–0 to formally rescind the June 5, 2019 transfer motion**:
> *"Commissioner O’Meara made a motion, seconded by Commissioner Lauter, to rescind a prior Motion made at the June 5, 2019 Special Meeting... The Motion approved was to Transfer a parcel Land comprising approximately nineteen (19) acres to the Town of Hingham... That Motion is now rescinded and the land in question shall remain in the ownership of the Hingham Housing Authority. The Motion was passed on a 4–0 vote."*

This vote extinguished the inter-municipal dispute and confirmed HHA's fee simple title.

---

## 3. Disentangling the Center for Active Living (CAL) vs. 100 Beal Street

A critical municipal distinction must be maintained between Hingham's two prominent senior initiatives:

| Dimension | Center for Active Living (CAL / HCAL) | 100 Beal Street Senior Affordable Housing |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Facility Type** | **Municipal Community Center:** Non-residential civic facility for recreation, health clinics, and Council on Aging offices. | **Residential Rental Housing:** 68 deed-restricted permanent apartment homes for low- and moderate-income seniors (age 62+). |
| **Sponsoring Authority** | **Town of Hingham:** Select Board, Council on Aging, and CAL Building Committee. | **Hingham Housing Authority (HHA):** Independent body corporate and politic under M.G.L. c. 121B. |
| **Siting Location** | Bare Cove Park Drive (Map 69, Parcel 44; 0 Fort Hill St). | 100 Beal Street (Map 58, Lot 23; School Tract II). |
| **Capital Financing** | **Municipal Bond Indebtedness:** $29.93M debt exclusion funded by local property taxes. | **Private/State Affordable Housing Capital:** Federal LIHTC equity, MassHousing, and state subsidy bonds. |
| **Town Meeting Outcome** | **DEFEATED:** April 27, 2026 ATM Article 12 failed the 2/3 debt threshold (**510 Yes to 470 No; 52.0%**). | **ACTIVE:** Town Meeting vote not required; HHA awarded development to Peabody Properties in August 2025. |

Lot A (~2.4 acres) houses the adolescent facility, and Lot C (~3.55 acres) is woodland buffer. | HHA RFP Section I & Attachment E; Plan Book 17, Page 508. |

| **Chapter 40B Status** | Described as an active ZBA docket | **No Chapter 40B application has been filed** with the Hingham ZBA as of September 2026. The project is in the pre-application design and state subsidy eligibility phase. | Town of Hingham ZBA Pending Applications Repository, September 2026. |
| **ORAD Recording** | Cited only as an unrecorded Conservation Commission vote | Formally recorded on **December 10, 2024 at Book 59527, Page 273** (Doc #78968). | Plymouth County Registry of Deeds, Book 59527, Page 273; MassDEP File No. 034-1509. |

---

## 5. Zoning, Chapter 40B Permitting & Safe Harbor Dynamics

* **Official & Open Space (OO) Zoning:** Multi-family residential housing is barred as of right in OO zoning districts, necessitating a Comprehensive Permit under **M.G.L. c. 40B** before the Hingham Zoning Board of Appeals (ZBA).
* **Safe Harbor Benchmark (760 CMR 56.03):** Because Hingham's Subsidized Housing Inventory (SHI) exceeds 10%, the Town has statutory safe harbor. The ZBA has legal latitude to condition or reject hostile 40B petitions. Therefore, 100 Beal Street is proceeding as a **negotiated "friendly" 40B** with broad municipal alignment.
* **Abutter Challenge:** Craftsman Village abutters (led by Shannon Brinkley) have urged the Town to invoke its Safe Harbor leverage to scale the project down from Peabody's 68 units to the 46-unit concept originally submitted by competing bidder AHSC.

---

## 6. Conservation Adjudication & Weymouth Back River ACEC

* **Order of Resource Area Delineation (ORAD):** Approved 5–0 on Nov 4, 2024; issued Nov 7, 2024; recorded Dec 10, 2024 at **Book 59527, Page 273** under **MassDEP File No. 034-1509** (Lucas Environmental & Control Point Associates).
* **Resource Envelope:** Three BVW systems, two IVWs, certified vernal pool, and 200-ft Riverfront Area.
* **Back River ACEC Watershed:** Discharges into Hockley Run and Tucker's Swamp tributary to the 1982 Weymouth Back River ACEC, subjecting stormwater designs to heightened water quality, volume, and thermal discharge scrutiny.
